    69→Recent for v1.1:

    72→- rootFolderKey DB copy kept as permanent fallback (never drop column, IPFS copy for recovery independence)
    73→- BYO-IPFS affects pinning only, all IPNS publishes still route through CipherBox API

    75→- IPFS/IPNS histogram buckets: 1ms-30s exponential (14 buckets); republish batch: 1s-120s (10 buckets)
    76→- Source label (db/network) only for resolve operations; empty string for pin/cat/publish
    77→- Alloy scrapes Kubo directly via Docker internal network (ipfs:5001), not proxied through API
    78→- Kubo Health dashboard panels use fallback Go runtime metrics alongside libp2p metrics pending post-deploy verification
    79→- IPNS-specific histograms: resolve 50ms-30s, publish 100ms-60s with source/outcome labels
    80→- Null resolve results (not found) excluded from IPNS histogram observations

    82→- sdk-core IPFS ops use direct axios/fetch (not api-client) for upload progress; IPNS ops use api-client generated functions
    83→- Bin/share operations take explicit context objects (BinOperationContext, ShareOperationContext) instead of Zustand stores
    84→- Share module accepts callback functions for API calls to stay transport-decoupled
    85→- Moved @cipherbox/core from dependencies to devDependencies in crypto (test-only cross-package assertions)
    86→- Kubo pebbleds datastore (LSM-tree) configured via IPFS_PROFILE=server,pebbleds; requires fresh volume on deploy
    87→- SDK concurrent pins require pebbleds datastore (synergistic); concurrent pins alone cause regression at 50 clients
    88→- Combined per-task commits into single commit due to pre-commit hook requiring api-client regeneration with entity/dto/controller changes
    89→- Desktop root folder detected by inode::ROOT_INO at publish call sites (simpler than modifying build_folder_metadata return type)
    90→- Desktop initialize_vault produces v2 blob for new users from day one (not just on migration)
    91→- decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public transparently handles both v1 JSON and v2 binary blobs
    92→- VaultExportDto returns only rootIpnsName and derivationMethod (crypto columns dropped)
    93→- Recovery tool IPNS resolution uses gateway /ipns/ HEAD request with redirect following (most reliable without API dependency)
    94→- fetchAndDecryptMetadata handles both v1 JSON and v2 binary blobs transparently for folder sync
    95→- Zero-crypto vault schema: server stores only ownerPublicKey and rootIpnsName, all crypto material lives exclusively in IPFS v2 blobs
    96→- DB crypto columns (encrypted_root_folder_key, encrypted_root_ipns_private_key, migrated_at) fully dropped — no fallback paths
    97→- Cargo workspace with centralized deps at repo root; cipherbox-crypto crate as foundation for all Rust SDK extraction
    98→- Module re-export pattern in desktop crypto/mod.rs preserves all existing crate::crypto::\* paths without touching call sites
    99→- cipherbox-core crate layered on cipherbox-crypto: folder, file, bin, vault_blob, ipns, registry, decrypt, error modules
   100→- File module re-exports FileMetadata types from folder.rs (shared AES encryption context with parent folder key)
   101→- decrypt module moved from fuse to crypto re-export (domain logic, not FUSE-specific)
   102→- Hand-structured API client crate rather than openapi-generator (modest API surface, proven code, no Java/Docker CI dependency)
   103→- critical-section std feature required for standalone ecies linking (Tauri provides it in desktop builds)

     1→# Roadmap: CipherBox v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→CipherBox v1.1 transforms the platform from "IPFS as a storage backend with database fallbacks" to "IPFS-native with the database serving only auth." The milestone establishes performance baselines before making changes, replaces the unreliable delegated-ipfs.dev dependency with self-hosted IPNS resolution, migrates rootFolderKey to an IPFS vault blob (achieving true zero-knowledge server), adds BYO-IPFS node support for data sovereignty, and completes performance baselines with client-side instrumentation and load testing after all features are stable.
     6→
     7→## Milestones
     8→
     9→- **v0.1 Staging MVP** - Phases 1-10 (shipped 2026-02-11)
    10→- **v1.0 Production** - Phases 11-17.1 (shipped 2026-03-05)
    11→- **v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure** - Phases 18-22 (in progress)
    12→
    13→## Phases
    14→
    15→**Phase Numbering:**
    16→
    17→- Integer phases (1, 2, 3): Planned milestone work
    18→- Decimal phases (2.1, 2.2): Urgent insertions (marked with INSERTED)
    19→
    20→Decimal phases appear between their surrounding integers in numeric order.
    21→
    22→- [x] **Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation** - Server-side Prometheus histograms and Kubo metrics scraping to establish baselines before any architectural changes (completed 2026-03-07)
    23→- [x] **Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement** - Replace delegated-ipfs.dev with self-hosted Someguy sidecar for reliable IPNS routing, add latency histograms for resolve/publish operations (completed 2026-03-07)
    24→- [x] **Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization** - Optimize Kubo pinning path (concurrent pins, worker tuning, pin batching) to reduce upload latency (INSERTED) (completed 2026-03-23)
    25→- [x] **Phase 20: Vault Migration** - Move rootFolderKey to IPFS vault blob v2 format, making the server store zero crypto material (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-24)
    26→- [ ] **Phase 21: BYO-IPFS Node Support** - User-configurable IPFS pinning endpoint with dual-pin strategy, Settings UI, and connection testing
    27→- [ ] **Phase 22: Performance Baselines Completion** - Client-side timing instrumentation, end-to-end journey timing, k6 load testing, and capacity documentation
    28→- [ ] **Phase 23: Rust SDK Extraction** - Extract five Rust crates (crypto, core, api-client, fuse, sdk) mirroring the TypeScript SDK hierarchy, replace duplicated logic in desktop FUSE code, enable unit testing at same granularity as TypeScript (gap closure in progress)
    29→
    30→## Phase Details
    31→
    32→### Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation
    33→
    34→**Goal**: Operators can observe IPFS/IPNS latency and API performance in Prometheus/Grafana before any architectural changes are made
    35→**Depends on**: Nothing (first phase of milestone)
    36→**Requirements**: PERF-01, PERF-02, PERF-03, PERF-04
    37→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
    38→
    39→1. Prometheus exposes duration histograms for IPNS resolve, IPNS publish, IPFS pin, and IPFS cat operations with per-operation labels
    40→2. API endpoint response times are captured at p50/p95/p99 for all critical routes (auth, IPNS resolve/publish, file upload/download, folder CRUD)
    41→3. Kubo node health metrics (peer count, bandwidth, datastore size) are visible in Prometheus via scraped Kubo endpoint
    42→4. TEE republish batch duration histogram captures per-batch timing with success/failure labels
    43→
    44→**Plans:** 2/2 plans complete
    45→
    46→Plans:
    47→
    48→- [x] 18-01-PLAN.md -- Add IPFS/IPNS + TEE duration histograms to MetricsService and instrument service/controller timing
    49→- [x] 18-02-PLAN.md -- Add Kubo scrape to Alloy, extend Grafana dashboard, create baseline benchmark script
    50→
    51→### Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement
    52→
    53→**Goal**: Users experience reliable, fast IPNS resolution without dependency on external delegated-ipfs.dev service
    54→**Depends on**: Phase 18 (baselines must exist to measure improvement)
    55→**Requirements**: IPNS-01, IPNS-02, IPNS-03, IPNS-04
    56→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
    57→
    58→1. Self-hosted Someguy is deployed alongside Kubo and serves as the IPNS routing provider, with delegated-ipfs.dev fully removed from the resolution path
    59→2. IPNS resolution completes within 2 seconds in the normal case (DB-first with async DHT verification) and degrades gracefully to DB-only when DHT is slow
    60→3. The standalone recovery tool resolves IPNS records via self-hosted Someguy without depending on the CipherBox API or delegated-ipfs.dev
    61→4. No user-visible errors or stale metadata when the DHT is temporarily unreachable (DB fallback serves correct data)
    62→   **Plans**: 2 plans
    63→
    64→Plans:
    65→
    66→- [x] 19-01-PLAN.md -- Deploy Someguy Docker sidecar, update routing URL in deploy workflow and .env.example
    67→- [x] 19-02-PLAN.md -- Add IPNS resolve/publish latency histograms and instrument IpnsService with timing
    68→
    69→### Phase 19.1: Extract core crypto SDK as shared package (INSERTED)
    70→
    71→**Goal:** Web app's crypto and file operation logic is extracted into a five-package layered SDK architecture (@cipherbox/crypto, @cipherbox/core, @cipherbox/api-client, @cipherbox/sdk-core, @cipherbox/sdk) enabling load testing, integration testing, and future CLI usage without a browser context
    72→**Requirements**: SDK-01, SDK-02, SDK-03, SDK-04, SDK-05, SDK-06, SDK-07, SDK-08, SDK-09, SDK-10, SDK-11
    73→**Depends on:** Phase 19
    74→**Plans:** 6/6 plans complete
    75→
    76→Plans:
    77→
    78→- [ ] 19.1-01-PLAN.md -- Split crypto into crypto + core packages with transitional re-exports
    79→- [ ] 19.1-02-PLAN.md -- Expand api-client package with orval generation and configurable instance
    80→- [ ] 19.1-03-PLAN.md -- Create sdk-core with stateless folder/file/IPFS/IPNS operations
    81→- [ ] 19.1-04-PLAN.md -- Create sdk with stateful CipherBoxClient, events, bin, and share operations
    82→- [ ] 19.1-05-PLAN.md -- Rewire web app hooks and stores to use SDK
    83→- [ ] 19.1-06-PLAN.md -- Remove re-exports, update imports, configure Release Please
    84→
    85→### Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization (INSERTED)
    86→
    87→**Goal:** Upload operations are measurably faster by optimizing the Kubo IPFS pinning path — the dominant bottleneck consuming ~95% of upload endpoint latency (~1.73s mean per pin, 3 sequential pins per upload)
    88→**Depends on:** Phase 19.1 (SDK needed for load test benchmarking)
    89→**Requirements**: PERF-09
    90→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
    91→
    92→1. Independent pin operations within a single upload (ciphertext + file metadata) execute concurrently rather than sequentially, reducing per-upload server-side time from ~5.4s to ~3.5s at p50
    93→2. Kubo pin worker configuration is tuned for concurrent load — pin latency under 50 concurrent clients does not exceed 2x the single-client baseline
    94→3. Load test results (same mixed workload scenario, same staging hardware) show measurable throughput improvement over Phase 19 baselines (>15% ops/s increase at 50+ clients)
    95→4. Latency improvements are validated with before/after Prometheus histogram comparisons and documented in baselines
    96→
    97→**Plans:** 4/4 plans complete
    98→
    99→Plans:
   100→
   101→- [x] 19.2-01-PLAN.md -- Capture pre-optimization baselines, parallelize SDK upload pin orchestration with Promise.allSettled
   102→- [x] 19.2-02-PLAN.md -- Switch Kubo datastore to pebbleds, capture post-optimization baselines with before/after comparison
   103→- [x] 19.2-03-PLAN.md -- Register PERF-09 in REQUIREMENTS.md (gap closure)
   104→- [x] 19.2-04-PLAN.md -- Three-point local performance baselines with matched-environment comparison (gap closure)
   105→
   106→### Phase 20: Vault Migration
   107→
   108→**Goal**: The server stores zero crypto material -- rootFolderKey lives in the IPFS vault blob, making the server a true zero-knowledge relay
   109→**Depends on**: Phase 19 (IPNS must be reliable before making it a login-adjacent dependency)
   110→**Requirements**: VAULT-01, VAULT-02, VAULT-03, VAULT-04, VAULT-05, VAULT-06
   111→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
   112→
   113→1. New users get vault blob v2 format on first login, with rootFolderKey ECIES-wrapped in the blob header and readable without any database round-trip
   114→2. All vaults use v2 format — DB crypto columns (encryptedRootFolderKey, encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey, migratedAt) dropped entirely
   115→3. The standalone recovery tool parses vault blob v2 and extracts rootFolderKey without needing the CipherBox API or database
   116→4. The desktop app (Rust) parses vault blob v2 and uses the embedded rootFolderKey for FUSE mount initialization
   117→5. The encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey column is dropped from the vaults table (HKDF derivation is the canonical path)
   118→
   119→**Plans:** 6/6 plans complete
   120→
   121→Plans:
   122→
   123→- [x] 20-01-PLAN.md -- Vault blob v2 format module in @cipherbox/core with TDD tests and cross-platform test vectors
   124→- [x] 20-02-PLAN.md -- API: DB migration, nullable columns, optional IPNS key on init, POST /vault/migrate endpoint
   125→- [x] 20-03-PLAN.md -- Desktop Rust: v2 blob module, vault fetch for migrated users, root folder v2 publish
   126→- [x] 20-04-PLAN.md -- Web client: v2 blob login read, lazy migration trigger, recovery tool v2 parsing
   127→- [ ] 20-05-PLAN.md -- Gap closure: API cleanup -- drop crypto columns, remove migrate endpoint, simplify DTOs
   128→- [ ] 20-06-PLAN.md -- Gap closure: Client cleanup -- remove dead migration paths, add v2 blob publish on new user init
   129→
   130→### Phase 21: BYO-IPFS Node Support
   131→
   132→**Goal**: Users can configure their own IPFS node for data sovereignty, with files pinned to both CipherBox and their personal node
   133→**Depends on**: Phase 19 (stable IPNS resolution benefits BYO workflows)
   134→**Requirements**: BYO-01, BYO-02, BYO-03, BYO-04, BYO-05, BYO-06, BYO-07
   135→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
   136→
   137→1. A user can enter their IPFS node endpoint and credentials in Settings, test the connection, and see a success/failure result
   138→2. After configuring a BYO node, every file upload is pinned to both the CipherBox node (always) and the user's node (best-effort mirror)
   139→3. All IPNS publishes still route through the CipherBox API regardless of BYO configuration (no bypass of optimistic concurrency)
   140→4. BYO users see an advisory quota display (not enforced) with clear indication that storage is managed by their own node
   141→5. The connection test endpoint validates reachability and API compatibility of the user's node before saving configuration
   142→   **Plans**: TBD
   143→
   144→### Phase 22: Performance Baselines Completion
   145→
   146→**Goal**: Complete performance picture exists -- client-side timing, end-to-end journeys, load test results, and capacity recommendations are documented
   147→**Depends on**: Phase 21 (all features must be stable to produce meaningful baselines)
   148→**Requirements**: PERF-05, PERF-06, PERF-07, PERF-08
   149→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
   150→
   151→1. Client-side timing instrumentation captures encrypt/decrypt throughput, upload/download duration, and IPNS operation timing in the browser
   152→2. End-to-end user journey timings are captured for login-to-vault, upload-to-visible, and share-to-accessible workflows
   153→3. k6 load test scripts simulate concurrent users performing upload, download, publish, and resolve operations with documented pass/fail thresholds
   154→4. Capacity thresholds are documented with scaling recommendations (max concurrent users, storage growth projections, IPNS publish throughput limits)
   155→   **Plans**: TBD
   156→
   157→### Phase 23: Rust SDK Extraction
   158→
   159→**Goal:** Extract five Rust crates (`cipherbox-crypto`, `cipherbox-core`, `cipherbox-api-client`, `cipherbox-fuse`, `cipherbox-sdk`) mirroring the TypeScript SDK package hierarchy. Replace duplicated crypto/IPNS/metadata logic in desktop FUSE code with crate imports. Enable unit testing at the same granularity as TypeScript. Desktop app becomes a thin Tauri shell.
   160→**Requirements**: RSDK-01, RSDK-02, RSDK-03, RSDK-04, RSDK-05, RSDK-06, RSDK-07, RSDK-08, RSDK-09, RSDK-10
   161→**Depends on:** None (can run independently alongside other phases)
   162→**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
   163→
   164→1. Five Rust crates compile independently under a Cargo workspace with centralized dependency versions
   165→2. Desktop app is a thin Tauri shell (~1,500 LOC) with all logic delegated to workspace crates
   166→3. Cross-language test vectors in `tests/vectors/` produce identical output in both Rust and TypeScript
   167→4. CI runs workspace-level builds on macOS, Linux, and Windows with cross-language parity gate
   168→5. No duplicated crypto, domain, or API logic remains in the desktop app
   169→
   170→**Plans:** 8 plans (7 complete, 1 gap closure)
   171→
   172→Plans:
   173→
   174→- [ ] 23-01-PLAN.md -- Cargo workspace scaffold + cipherbox-crypto crate extraction
   175→- [ ] 23-02-PLAN.md -- cipherbox-core crate extraction (domain types, metadata, IPNS records)
   176→- [ ] 23-03-PLAN.md -- cipherbox-api-client crate + shared test vectors extraction
   177→- [ ] 23-04-PLAN.md -- cipherbox-fuse crate extraction (platform-agnostic + platform modules)
   178→- [x] 23-05-PLAN.md -- cipherbox-sdk crate extraction (sync, queue, state, registry)
   179→- [ ] 23-06-PLAN.md -- Desktop app thin shell cleanup + full workspace verification
   180→- [ ] 23-07-PLAN.md -- CI workspace builds + Release Please + cross-language parity gate
   181→- [ ] 23-08-PLAN.md -- Gap closure: Move Windows WinFsp operations to crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/
   182→
   183→## Progress
   184→
   185→**Execution Order:**
   186→Phases execute in numeric order: 18 -> 19 -> 19.1 -> 19.2 -> 20 -> 21 -> 22

   139→
   140→<frontmatter_guidance>
   141→**Purpose:** Enable automatic context assembly via dependency graph. Frontmatter makes summary metadata machine-readable so plan-phase can scan all summaries quickly and select relevant ones based on dependencies.
   142→
   143→**Fast scanning:** Frontmatter is first ~25 lines, cheap to scan across all summaries without reading full content.
   144→
   145→**Dependency graph:** `requires`/`provides`/`affects` create explicit links between phases, enabling transitive closure for context selection.
   146→
   147→**Subsystem:** Primary categorization (auth, payments, ui, api, database, infra, testing) for detecting related phases.
   148→
   149→**Tags:** Searchable technical keywords (libraries, frameworks, tools) for tech stack awareness.
   150→
   151→**Key-files:** Important files for @context references in PLAN.md.
   152→
   153→**Patterns:** Established conventions future phases should maintain.
   154→
   155→**Population:** Frontmatter is populated during summary creation in execute-plan.md. See `<step name="create_summary">` for field-by-field guidance.
   156→</frontmatter_guidance>
   157→
   158→<one_liner_rules>
   159→The one-liner MUST be substantive:
   160→
   161→**Good:**
   162→
   163→- "JWT auth with refresh rotation using jose library"
   164→- "Prisma schema with User, Session, and Product models"
   165→- "Dashboard with real-time metrics via Server-Sent Events"
   166→
   167→**Bad:**
   168→
   169→- "Phase complete"
   170→- "Authentication implemented"
   171→- "Foundation finished"
   172→- "All tasks done"
   173→
   174→The one-liner should tell someone what actually shipped.
   175→</one_liner_rules>
   176→
   177→<example>
   178→```markdown
   179→# Phase 1: Foundation Summary
   180→
   181→**JWT auth with refresh rotation using jose library, Prisma User model, and protected API middleware**
   182→
   183→## Performance
   184→
   185→- **Duration:** 28 min
   186→- **Started:** 2025-01-15T14:22:10Z
   187→- **Completed:** 2025-01-15T14:50:33Z
   188→- **Tasks:** 5
   189→- **Files modified:** 8
   190→
   191→## Accomplishments
   192→
   193→- User model with email/password auth
   194→- Login/logout endpoints with httpOnly JWT cookies
   195→- Protected route middleware checking token validity
   196→- Refresh token rotation on each request
   197→
   198→## Files Created/Modified
   199→
   200→- `prisma/schema.prisma` - User and Session models
   201→- `src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts` - Login endpoint
   202→- `src/app/api/auth/logout/route.ts` - Logout endpoint
   203→- `src/middleware.ts` - Protected route checks
   204→- `src/lib/auth.ts` - JWT helpers using jose
   205→
   206→## Decisions Made
   207→
   208→- Used jose instead of jsonwebtoken (ESM-native, Edge-compatible)
   209→- 15-min access tokens with 7-day refresh tokens
   210→- Storing refresh tokens in database for revocation capability
   211→
   212→## Deviations from Plan
   213→
   214→### Auto-fixed Issues
   215→
   216→**1. [Rule 2 - Missing Critical] Added password hashing with bcrypt**
   217→
   218→- **Found during:** Task 2 (Login endpoint implementation)
   219→- **Issue:** Plan didn't specify password hashing - storing plaintext would be critical security flaw
   220→- **Fix:** Added bcrypt hashing on registration, comparison on login with salt rounds 10
   221→- **Files modified:** src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts, src/lib/auth.ts
   222→- **Verification:** Password hash test passes, plaintext never stored
   223→- **Committed in:** abc123f (Task 2 commit)
   224→
   225→**2. [Rule 3 - Blocking] Installed missing jose dependency**
   226→
   227→- **Found during:** Task 4 (JWT token generation)
   228→- **Issue:** jose package not in package.json, import failing
   229→- **Fix:** Ran `npm install jose`
   230→- **Files modified:** package.json, package-lock.json
   231→- **Verification:** Import succeeds, build passes
   232→- **Committed in:** def456g (Task 4 commit)
   233→
   234→---
   235→
   236→**Total deviations:** 2 auto-fixed (1 missing critical, 1 blocking)
   237→**Impact on plan:** Both auto-fixes essential for security and functionality. No scope creep.
   238→
   239→## Issues Encountered
   240→
   241→- jsonwebtoken CommonJS import failed in Edge runtime - switched to jose (planned library change, worked as expected)
   242→
   243→## Next Phase Readiness
   244→
   245→- Auth foundation complete, ready for feature development
   246→- User registration endpoint needed before public launch
   247→
   248→---
   249→
   250→_Phase: 01-foundation_
   251→_Completed: 2025-01-15_
   252→
   253→```
   254→</example>
   255→
   256→<guidelines>
   257→**Frontmatter:** MANDATORY - complete all fields. Enables automatic context assembly for future planning.
   258→
   259→**One-liner:** Must be substantive. "JWT auth with refresh rotation using jose library" not "Authentication implemented".
   260→

   263→- Extracted to STATE.md accumulated context
   264→- Use "None - followed plan as specified" if no deviations
   265→

{
  "description": "Security review Phase 23 Rust crates",
  "subagent_type": "security-reviewer",
  "prompt": "\n# Security Review: Phase 23 — Rust SDK Extraction\n\n## Context\n\nPhase 23 extracted 5 Rust crates from the CipherBox desktop app:\n- `crates/crypto/` — cipherbox-crypto: AES-256-GCM encryption, ECIES key wrapping, HKDF derivation, Ed25519 IPNS key derivation\n- `crates/core/` — cipherbox-core: domain types, metadata serialization, vault blob v2 parsing\n- `crates/api-client/` — cipherbox-api-client: HTTP client for CipherBox API\n- `crates/fuse/` — cipherbox-fuse: FUSE filesystem with platform modules (macOS, Linux, Windows)\n- `crates/sdk/` — cipherbox-sdk: sync daemon, upload queue, state management\n\nThe desktop app (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/`) was reduced to a thin Tauri shell delegating to these crates.\n\n## Project Security Rules (from CLAUDE.md)\n- Never store privateKey in localStorage/sessionStorage\n- Never log sensitive keys\n- Never send unencrypted keys to server\n- Always use ECIES for key wrapping\n- Always use AES-256-GCM for content encryption\n- Server NEVER has access to plaintext or unencrypted keys\n- Always encrypt ipnsPrivateKey with TEE public key before sending\n- TEE decrypts IPNS keys in hardware only, signs, and immediately discards\n\n## What to Review\n\n### 1. crates/crypto/ — HIGHEST PRIORITY\nReview ALL files in `crates/crypto/src/`. This is the cryptographic foundation. Check:\n- AES-256-GCM: correct nonce generation (random 12 bytes), nonce uniqueness, authenticated encryption\n- ECIES: proper key wrapping implementation\n- HKDF: correct derivation with appropriate salt/info\n- Ed25519: IPNS key derivation correctness\n- Key material handling: zeroization after use, no logging, no leaks in errors\n- Dependency choices: are the crypto libraries (aes-gcm, ecies, hkdf, ed25519-dalek, etc.) reputable and current?\n\n### 2. crates/core/ — HIGH PRIORITY\nReview `crates/core/src/`. Check:\n- Vault blob v2 parsing: buffer handling, length validation, no panics on malformed input\n- Metadata serialization: no plaintext leaks in serialized format\n- Key hierarchy: folderKey derivation follows spec (rootFolderKey → folderKey → fileKey)\n- Error types: no sensitive data in error messages\n\n### 3. crates/api-client/ — MEDIUM PRIORITY\nReview `crates/api-client/src/`. Check:\n- Auth token handling: tokens not logged\n- HTTPS enforcement or at least not hardcoded HTTP\n- No key material passed in URL query params\n- Request/response handling: no plaintext key exposure\n\n### 4. crates/fuse/ — MEDIUM PRIORITY\nReview `crates/fuse/src/` focusing on:\n- `lib.rs` — main FUSE filesystem, encryption/decryption operations\n- How keys are stored in memory (CipherBoxFS struct)\n- File content encryption/decryption flow\n- Metadata encryption before IPNS publish\n- Any plaintext caching (are decrypted files cached on disk?)\n\n### 5. crates/sdk/ — MEDIUM PRIORITY\nReview `crates/sdk/src/`. Check:\n- Key state management\n- Sync daemon: does it expose keys?\n- Upload queue: encrypted before queuing?\n\n### 6. Desktop thin shell\nCheck `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/` for:\n- Key material in Tauri commands (exposed to frontend)\n- Keychain operations\n- Any remaining crypto that should have moved to crates\n\n## Specific Vulnerability Patterns to Check\n\n1. **Nonce reuse**: Are AES-GCM nonces always freshly generated? Never reused with same key?\n2. **Key zeroization**: Do Rust types implement `Zeroize` or `Drop` with zeroing for key material?\n3. **Panic safety**: Can malformed ciphertext cause panics (unwrap on decrypt)?\n4. **Timing attacks**: Constant-time comparison for authentication?\n5. **Error oracle**: Do error messages distinguish between \"wrong key\" vs \"corrupted data\" (padding oracle equivalent)?\n6. **Unsafe code**: Any `unsafe` blocks in crypto paths?\n7. **Dependency audit**: Check Cargo.toml for known-vulnerable versions\n\n## Output Format\n\nWrite the complete review report to `.planning/security/REVIEW-2026-03-24-phase23.md` following this structure:\n\n```markdown\n# Security Review Report\n\n**Date:** 2026-03-24\n**Scope:** Phase 23 — Rust SDK Extraction (5 crates + desktop thin shell)\n**Reviewer:** Claude (security:review command)\n\n## Executive Summary\n[2-3 sentences]\n**Risk Level:** [LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL]\n\n## Files Reviewed\n[table of files, operations, risk]\n\n## Findings\n### Critical Issues\n### High Priority\n### Medium Priority  \n### Low Priority / Recommendations\n\n## Detailed Analysis\n[per-crate analysis with file:line references]\n\n## Test Cases\n[concrete Rust test suggestions]\n\n## Compliance Checklist\n[against project security rules]\n\n## Recommendations Summary\n[prioritized table]\n```\n\nBe thorough. Every finding must have specific file:line references. Generate implementable test cases.\n"
}

```
## SECURITY REVIEW COMPLETE

**Files analyzed:** ~55 Rust source files across 6 code areas
**Crypto operations found:** ~30 distinct operations (AES-GCM, AES-CTR, ECIES, HKDF, Ed25519, IPNS record signing)
**Issues found:** 0 Critical, 2 High, 4 Medium, 5 Low

### Critical Issues

None found. The cryptographic architecture is sound and follows the project's zero-knowledge security model.

### High Priority

1. **AES-CTR lacks authentication** -- CTR-mode files rely on IPFS CID for integrity, but the client does not verify the CID after fetching bytes. An attacker controlling the API/IPFS relay could serve tampered ciphertext with predictable bit-flip impact. Fix: add client-side CID verification before decryption.

2. **`generate_ed25519_keypair` returns private key as plain `Vec<u8>`** -- not wrapped in `Zeroizing`, leaving key material in freed memory. Fix: return `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` for the private key.

### Test Cases Generated

~30 test suggestions across 6 categories: AES-GCM (6 tests), AES-CTR (4 tests), ECIES (5 tests), HKDF (4 tests), Ed25519 (4 tests), Core/VaultBlob (5 tests)

### Report Location

`.planning/security/REVIEW-2026-03-24-phase23.md`

### Recommendations

1. **Add `zeroize` feature to `ed25519-dalek`** in workspace `Cargo.toml` -- trivial change, ensures `SigningKey` zeroes on drop (MEDIUM-1)
2. **Wrap `generate_ed25519_keypair` return in `Zeroizing`** -- low effort, closes HIGH-2
3. **Add client-side CID verification for CTR-mode files** -- medium effort, closes HIGH-1
4. **Fix registry `app_version` copy-paste bug** at `crates/sdk/src/registry.rs:102` -- trivial, closes LOW-4

### Key Positive Findings

- All key material in `KeyState` and `CipherBoxFS` uses `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` wrappers
- `KeyState::clear()` properly zeroizes all sensitive fields on logout
- `CipherBoxFS::handle_destroy()` zeroizes all caches, pending content, and open file handles
- API client types have custom `Debug` impls that redact tokens and keys
- Error messages are generic throughout the crypto crate (no oracle risk)
- `OsRng` (CSPRNG) used for all random generation
- HKDF domain separation is correct across vault, vault-key, registry, bin, and per-file derivation paths
- Temp files use `0o600` permissions and zero-overwrite cleanup

 SECURITY REVIEW COMPLETE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

**Scope:** Phase 23 — Rust SDK Extraction (5 crates + desktop thin shell)
**Risk Level:** MEDIUM
**Files analyzed:** ~55 Rust source files

| Severity | Count |
|----------|-------|
| Critical | 0 |
| High | 2 |
| Medium | 4 |
| Low | 5 |

## Top Issues

1. **HIGH — AES-CTR lacks client-side integrity verification.** CTR-mode decryption relies on IPFS CID for integrity, but the client doesn't verify the CID after fetching bytes. An attacker controlling the relay could serve tampered ciphertext with predictable bit-flip impact.

2. **HIGH — `generate_ed25519_keypair` returns bare `Vec<u8>`.** Private key material not wrapped in `Zeroizing`, leaving it in freed memory after drop.

3. **MEDIUM — `ed25519-dalek` missing `zeroize` feature.** `SigningKey` doesn't zero on drop without the feature flag enabled in workspace Cargo.toml.

## Positive Observations

- All `KeyState` and `CipherBoxFS` key fields use `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`
- Proper zeroization on logout (`KeyState::clear()`) and unmount (`CipherBoxFS::handle_destroy()`)
- `OsRng` (CSPRNG) used everywhere — no `rand::thread_rng()` in crypto paths
- HKDF domain separation is correct across all 5 derivation contexts
- API client redacts tokens/keys in `Debug` impls
- Error messages are generic (no decrypt oracle risk)
- Temp files use `0o600` permissions with zero-overwrite cleanup

## Test Cases Generated

~30 test suggestions across AES-GCM, AES-CTR, ECIES, HKDF, Ed25519, and VaultBlob categories.

**Full report:** `.planning/security/REVIEW-2026-03-24-phase23.md`

     1→//! Ed25519 key generation, signing, and verification.
     2→//!
     3→//! Used for IPNS record signing. Deterministic signatures are critical
     4→//! for cross-language test vector verification.
     5→
     6→use ed25519_dalek::{Signer, SigningKey, Verifier, VerifyingKey};
     7→use rand::rngs::OsRng;
     8→use zeroize::Zeroize;
     9→
    10→use crate::error::CryptoError;
    11→
    12→/// Ed25519 public key size in bytes.
    13→pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_SIZE: usize = 32;
    14→
    15→/// Ed25519 private key size in bytes.
    16→pub const ED25519_PRIVATE_KEY_SIZE: usize = 32;
    17→
    18→/// Ed25519 signature size in bytes.
    19→pub const ED25519_SIGNATURE_SIZE: usize = 64;
    20→
    21→/// Generate a new Ed25519 keypair.
    22→///
    23→/// Returns (public_key_32bytes, private_key_32bytes).
    24→pub fn generate_ed25519_keypair() -> (Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>) {
    25→    let signing_key = SigningKey::generate(&mut OsRng);
    26→    let verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();
    27→
    28→    (
    29→        verifying_key.to_bytes().to_vec(),
    30→        signing_key.to_bytes().to_vec(),
    31→    )
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Sign a message with an Ed25519 private key.
    35→///
    36→/// Returns 64-byte deterministic signature.
    37→pub fn sign_ed25519(message: &[u8], private_key: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CryptoError> {
    38→    if private_key.len() != ED25519_PRIVATE_KEY_SIZE {
    39→        return Err(CryptoError::InvalidPrivateKey);
    40→    }
    41→
    42→    let mut key_bytes: [u8; 32] = private_key
    43→        .try_into()
    44→        .map_err(|_| CryptoError::InvalidPrivateKey)?;
    45→    let signing_key = SigningKey::from_bytes(&key_bytes);
    46→    key_bytes.zeroize();
    47→    let signature = signing_key.sign(message);
    48→
    49→    Ok(signature.to_bytes().to_vec())
    50→}
    51→
    52→/// Verify an Ed25519 signature.
    53→///
    54→/// Returns true if valid, false otherwise. Never throws.
    55→pub fn verify_ed25519(message: &[u8], signature: &[u8], public_key: &[u8]) -> bool {
    56→    if signature.len() != ED25519_SIGNATURE_SIZE || public_key.len() != ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_SIZE {
    57→        return false;
    58→    }
    59→
    60→    let Ok(sig_bytes) = <[u8; 64]>::try_from(signature) else {
    61→        return false;
    62→    };
    63→    let Ok(key_bytes) = <[u8; 32]>::try_from(public_key) else {
    64→        return false;
    65→    };
    66→
    67→    let Ok(verifying_key) = VerifyingKey::from_bytes(&key_bytes) else {
    68→        return false;
    69→    };
    70→
    71→    let sig = ed25519_dalek::Signature::from_bytes(&sig_bytes);
    72→    verifying_key.verify(message, &sig).is_ok()
    73→}
    74→
    75→/// Derive the 32-byte public key from a 32-byte Ed25519 private key.
    76→pub fn get_public_key(private_key: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CryptoError> {
    77→    if private_key.len() != ED25519_PRIVATE_KEY_SIZE {
    78→        return Err(CryptoError::InvalidPrivateKey);
    79→    }
    80→
    81→    let mut key_bytes: [u8; 32] = private_key
    82→        .try_into()
    83→        .map_err(|_| CryptoError::InvalidPrivateKey)?;
    84→    let signing_key = SigningKey::from_bytes(&key_bytes);
    85→    key_bytes.zeroize();
    86→    let verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();
    87→
    88→    Ok(verifying_key.to_bytes().to_vec())
    89→}

The `ipns_private_key` is already `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` now. The `.clone()` on line 472 will clone the inner `Vec<u8>` and re-wrap in `Zeroizing`, and the deref to `&[u8]` on line 500 works transparently. But we can simplify by removing the redundant `Zeroizing::new()` wrapper since the value is already `Zeroizing`.

Now I need to check the Windows write_ops line 146 where `ipns_private_key.try_into()` converts the `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` to `[u8; 32]`. The `Zeroizing` derefs to `Vec<u8>`, but `try_into()` for `Vec<u8> -> [u8; 32]` consumes the vec. With `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`, we need to handle this differently since `Zeroizing` doesn't implement `TryInto<[u8; 32]>` directly.

Line 146: `ipns_private_key.try_into()` — this moves the `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` into the thread. `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` implements `Deref<Target=Vec<u8>>` but `try_into()` consumes. We need to unwrap first with `into_inner()` or use `(*ipns_private_key).clone().try_into()`. Actually, let me check — `Zeroizing` implements `Into<T>` for its inner type? No, but the `move` closure takes ownership. Let me check if this actually needs a fix.

The `ipns_private_key` is `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`. When moved into the thread, we can use `ipns_private_key.into_inner()` (available since zeroize 1.5) or `Vec::from(ipns_private_key)`. Actually, `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` doesn't have a direct `TryInto<[u8; 32]>`. Let me fix this properly.

> AGENT

Done. Both security findings fixed in `7ff9ef2ff`:

1. **`ed25519-dalek` zeroize feature** — enabled in workspace `Cargo.toml`, so `SigningKey` now zeroes on drop
2. **`generate_ed25519_keypair` return type** — private key wrapped in `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`, callers updated to use directly instead of redundant `Zeroizing::new()`

All crypto tests pass, both crates compile clean.

    39→    pub fn block_with_timeout<F, T>(rt: &tokio::runtime::Handle, fut: F) -> Result<T, String>
    40→    where
    41→        F: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T, String>>,
    42→    {
    43→        rt.block_on(async {
    44→            match tokio::time::timeout(NETWORK_TIMEOUT, fut).await {
    45→                Ok(result) => result,
    46→                Err(_) => Err("Operation timed out".to_string()),

    49→    }

    51→    pub fn fetch_and_populate_folder(

    70→
    71→        let (encrypted_bytes, cid) = result;
    72→        let metadata = cipherbox_core::decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public(&encrypted_bytes, &folder_key_owned)?;
    73→        fs.metadata_cache.set(&ipns_name.to_string(), metadata.clone(), cid);
    74→        fs.inodes.populate_folder(ino, &metadata, &private_key, &fs.public_key, false)?;
    75→
    76→        let unresolved = fs.inodes.get_unresolved_file_pointers();
    77→        if !unresolved.is_empty() {
    78→            log::info!("Resolving {} FilePointer(s) for folder ino {}", unresolved.len(), ino);
    79→            resolve_file_pointers_blocking(fs, &unresolved, &folder_key_owned)?;
    80→        }
    81→
    82→        Ok(())
    83→    }
    84→
    85→    pub fn resolve_file_pointers_blocking(
    86→        fs: &mut CipherBoxFS,
    87→        unresolved: &[(u64, String)],
    88→        folder_key: &[u8],
    89→    ) -> Result<(), String> {
    90→        let api = fs.api.clone();
    91→        let rt = fs.rt.clone();
    92→        let folder_key_arr: [u8; 32] = folder_key.try_into()
    93→            .map_err(|_| "Invalid folder key length for FilePointer resolution".to_string())?;
    94→
    95→        for (ino, ipns_name) in unresolved {
    96→            let resolve_result = block_with_timeout(&rt, async {
    97→                let resp = cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(&api, ipns_name)
    98→                    .await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?;
    99→                cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::fetch_content(&api, &resp.cid)
   100→                    .await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))
   101→            });
   102→
   103→            match resolve_result {
   104→                Ok(encrypted_bytes) => {
   105→                    match cipherbox_core::decrypt_file_metadata_from_ipfs_public(&encrypted_bytes, &folder_key_arr) {
   106→                        Ok(file_meta) => {
   107→                            fs.inodes.resolve_file_pointer(
   108→                                *ino, file_meta.cid, file_meta.file_key_encrypted,
   109→                                file_meta.file_iv, file_meta.size, file_meta.encryption_mode,
   110→                                file_meta.versions,
   111→                            );
   112→                        }
   113→                        Err(e) => log::warn!("FilePointer resolution failed for ino {} ({}): {}", ino, ipns_name, e),
   114→                    }
   115→                }
   116→                Err(e) => log::warn!("FilePointer IPNS resolve failed for ino {} ({}): {}", ino, ipns_name, e),
   117→            }
   118→        }
   119→        Ok(())
   120→    }
   121→
   122→    pub fn fetch_and_decrypt_file_content(
   123→        fs: &CipherBoxFS,
   124→        cid: &str,
   125→        encrypted_file_key_hex: &str,
   126→        iv_hex: &str,
   127→        encryption_mode: &str,
   128→    ) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
   129→        let api = fs.api.clone();
   130→        let private_key = fs.private_key.clone();
   131→        let cid_owned = cid.to_string();
   132→        let key_hex = encrypted_file_key_hex.to_string();
   133→        let iv_hex_owned = iv_hex.to_string();
   134→        let mode = encryption_mode.to_string();
   135→        let rt = fs.rt.clone();
   136→
   137→        block_with_timeout(&rt, async {
   138→            let encrypted_bytes = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::fetch_content(&api, &cid_owned)
   139→                .await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?;
   140→            let encrypted_file_key = hex::decode(&key_hex).map_err(|_| "Invalid file key hex".to_string())?;
   141→            let file_key = zeroize::Zeroizing::new(
   142→                cipherbox_crypto::unwrap_key(&encrypted_file_key, &private_key)
   143→                    .map_err(|e| format!("File key unwrap failed: {}", e))?,
   144→            );
   145→            let file_key_arr: [u8; 32] = file_key.as_slice().try_into()
   146→                .map_err(|_| "Invalid file key length".to_string())?;
   147→
   148→            let plaintext = if mode == "CTR" {
   149→                let iv = hex::decode(&iv_hex_owned).map_err(|_| "Invalid file IV hex".to_string())?;
   150→                let iv_arr: [u8; 16] = iv.try_into().map_err(|_| "Invalid CTR IV length (expected 16)".to_string())?;
   151→                cipherbox_crypto::decrypt_aes_ctr(&encrypted_bytes, &file_key_arr, &iv_arr)
   152→                    .map_err(|e| format!("CTR file decryption failed: {}", e))?
   153→            } else {
   154→                let iv = hex::decode(&iv_hex_owned).map_err(|_| "Invalid file IV hex".to_string())?;
   155→                let iv_arr: [u8; 12] = iv.try_into().map_err(|_| "Invalid GCM IV length (expected 12)".to_string())?;
   156→                cipherbox_crypto::decrypt_aes_gcm(&encrypted_bytes, &file_key_arr, &iv_arr)
   157→                    .map_err(|e| format!("GCM file decryption failed: {}", e))?
   158→            };
   159→            Ok(plaintext)
   160→        })
   161→    }
   162→
   163→    pub async fn fetch_and_decrypt_content_async(
   164→        api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
   165→        cid: &str,
   166→        encrypted_file_key_hex: &str,
   167→        iv_hex: &str,
   168→        encryption_mode: &str,
   169→        private_key: &[u8],
   170→    ) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
   171→        let encrypted_bytes = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::fetch_content(api, cid)
   172→            .await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?;
   173→        let encrypted_file_key = hex::decode(encrypted_file_key_hex)
   174→            .map_err(|_| "Invalid file key hex".to_string())?;
   175→        let file_key = zeroize::Zeroizing::new(
   176→            cipherbox_crypto::unwrap_key(&encrypted_file_key, private_key)
   177→                .map_err(|e| format!("File key unwrap failed: {}", e))?,
   178→        );
   179→        let file_key_arr: [u8; 32] = file_key.as_slice().try_into()
   180→            .map_err(|_| "Invalid file key length".to_string())?;
   181→
   182→        let plaintext = if encryption_mode == "CTR" {
   183→            let iv = hex::decode(iv_hex).map_err(|_| "Invalid file IV hex".to_string())?;
   184→            let iv_arr: [u8; 16] = iv.try_into().map_err(|_| "Invalid CTR IV length (expected 16)".to_string())?;
   185→            cipherbox_crypto::decrypt_aes_ctr(&encrypted_bytes, &file_key_arr, &iv_arr)
   186→                .map_err(|e| format!("CTR decryption failed: {}", e))?
   187→        } else {
   188→            let iv = hex::decode(iv_hex).map_err(|_| "Invalid file IV hex".to_string())?;
   189→            let iv_arr: [u8; 12] = iv.try_into().map_err(|_| "Invalid GCM IV length (expected 12)".to_string())?;
   190→            cipherbox_crypto::decrypt_aes_gcm(&encrypted_bytes, &file_key_arr, &iv_arr)
   191→                .map_err(|e| format!("GCM decryption failed: {}", e))?
   192→        };
   193→        Ok(plaintext)
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    pub async fn publish_file_metadata(
   197→        api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
   198→        file_meta: &cipherbox_core::FileMetadata,
   199→        folder_key: &[u8],
   200→        file_ipns_private_key: &zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
   201→        file_ipns_name: &str,
   202→        coordinator: &crate::PublishCoordinator,
   203→    ) -> Result<(), String> {
   204→        let folder_key_arr: [u8; 32] = folder_key.try_into()
   205→            .map_err(|_| "Invalid folder key length for FileMetadata encryption".to_string())?;
   206→
   207→        let sealed = cipherbox_core::folder::encrypt_file_metadata(file_meta, &folder_key_arr)
   208→            .map_err(|e| format!("FileMetadata encryption failed: {}", e))?;
   209→
   210→        let iv_hex = hex::encode(&sealed[..12]);
   211→        use base64::Engine;
   212→        let data_base64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&sealed[12..]);
   213→        let json = serde_json::json!({ "iv": iv_hex, "data": data_base64 });
   214→        let json_bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&json)
   215→            .map_err(|e| format!("FileMetadata JSON serialization failed: {}", e))?;
   216→
   217→        let file_meta_cid = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::upload_content(api, &json_bytes)
   218→            .await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?;
   219→
   220→        let seq = coordinator.resolve_sequence(api, file_ipns_name).await?;
   221→
   222→        let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = file_ipns_private_key.as_slice().try_into()
   223→            .map_err(|_| "Invalid file IPNS private key length".to_string())?;
   224→        let new_seq = seq + 1;
   225→        let value = format!("/ipfs/{}", file_meta_cid);
   226→        let record = cipherbox_core::create_ipns_record(&ipns_key_arr, &value, new_seq, 86_400_000)
   227→            .map_err(|e| format!("File IPNS record creation failed: {}", e))?;
   228→        let marshaled = cipherbox_core::marshal_ipns_record(&record)
   229→            .map_err(|e| format!("File IPNS record marshal failed: {}", e))?;
   230→
   231→        let record_b64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&marshaled);
   232→
   233→        let req = cipherbox_api_client::IpnsPublishRequest {
   234→            ipns_name: file_ipns_name.to_string(),
   235→            record: record_b64,
   236→            metadata_cid: file_meta_cid.clone(),
   237→            encrypted_ipns_private_key: None,
   238→            key_epoch: None,
   239→            expected_sequence_number: None,
   240→        };
   241→        match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::publish_ipns(api, &req).await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))? {
   242→            cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Success => {}
   243→            cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Conflict { .. } => {
   244→                log::warn!("Unexpected conflict on per-file IPNS publish for {}", file_ipns_name);
   245→            }
   246→        }
   247→
   248→        coordinator.record_publish(file_ipns_name, new_seq);
   249→        log::info!("Per-file IPNS publish succeeded for {}", file_ipns_name);
   250→        Ok(())
   251→    }

   382→    pub fn fetch_and_populate_folder(
   383→        fs: &mut CipherBoxFS,
   384→        ino: u64,
   385→        ipns_name: &str,
   386→        folder_key: &[u8],

   394→        let result = crate::block_with_timeout(&rt, async {
   395→            let resolve_resp =
   396→                cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(&api, &ipns_name_owned).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   397→            let encrypted_bytes =
   398→                cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::fetch_content(&api, &resolve_resp.cid).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   399→            Ok::<(Vec<u8>, String), String>((encrypted_bytes, resolve_resp.cid))
   400→        })?;
   401→
   402→        let (encrypted_bytes, cid) = result;
   403→        let metadata =
   404→            cipherbox_core::decrypt::decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public(&encrypted_bytes, &folder_key_owned)?;
   405→        fs.metadata_cache
   406→            .set(&ipns_name.to_string(), metadata.clone(), cid);
   407→        fs.inodes.populate_folder(
   408→            ino,
   409→            &metadata,
   410→            &private_key,
   411→            &fs.public_key,
   412→            false,
   413→        )?;
   414→
   415→        // Resolve unresolved FilePointers eagerly
   416→        let unresolved = fs.inodes.get_unresolved_file_pointers();
   417→        if !unresolved.is_empty() {
   418→            log::info!(
   419→                "Resolving {} FilePointer(s) for folder ino {}",
   420→                unresolved.len(),
   421→                ino
   422→            );
   423→            resolve_file_pointers_blocking(fs, &unresolved, &folder_key_owned)?;
   424→        }
   425→
   426→        Ok(())
   427→    }
   428→
   429→    /// Resolve FilePointer inodes by fetching and decrypting per-file IPNS metadata.
   430→    pub fn resolve_file_pointers_blocking(
   431→        fs: &mut CipherBoxFS,
   432→        unresolved: &[(u64, String)],
   433→        folder_key: &[u8],
   434→    ) -> Result<(), String> {
   435→        let api = fs.api.clone();
   436→        let rt = fs.rt.clone();
   437→        let folder_key_arr: [u8; 32] = folder_key.try_into().map_err(|_| {
   438→            "Invalid folder key length for FilePointer resolution".to_string()
   439→        })?;
   440→
   441→        for (ino, ipns_name) in unresolved {
   442→            let resolve_result = crate::block_with_timeout(&rt, async {
   443→                let resp =
   444→                    cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(&api, ipns_name).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   445→                let encrypted_bytes =
   446→                    cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::fetch_content(&api, &resp.cid).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   447→                Ok::<Vec<u8>, String>(encrypted_bytes)
   448→            });
   449→
   450→            match resolve_result {
   451→                Ok(encrypted_bytes) => {
   452→                    match cipherbox_core::decrypt::decrypt_file_metadata_from_ipfs_public(
   453→                        &encrypted_bytes,
   454→                        &folder_key_arr,
   455→                    ) {
   456→                        Ok(file_meta) => {
   457→                            fs.inodes.resolve_file_pointer(
   458→                                *ino,
   459→                                file_meta.cid,
   460→                                file_meta.file_key_encrypted,
   461→                                file_meta.file_iv,
   462→                                file_meta.size,
   463→                                file_meta.encryption_mode,
   464→                                file_meta.versions,
   465→                            );
   466→                        }
   467→                        Err(e) => {
   468→                            log::warn!(
   469→                                "FilePointer resolution failed for ino {} ({}): {}",
   470→                                ino,
   471→                                ipns_name,
   472→                                e
   473→                            );
   474→                        }

  "command": "gh pr create --title \"feat: extract Rust SDK as five workspace crates (Phase 23)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- Extract five Rust crates (`cipherbox-crypto`, `cipherbox-core`, `cipherbox-api-client`, `cipherbox-fuse`, `cipherbox-sdk`) mirroring the TypeScript SDK hierarchy under a Cargo workspace\n- Replace all duplicated crypto, domain, and API logic in the desktop app with crate imports — desktop reduced to a thin Tauri shell\n- Add cross-language test vectors (`tests/vectors/`) with CI parity gate ensuring Rust and TypeScript produce identical crypto output\n- Configure Release Please for all 5 Rust crates with workspace-level version tracking\n- Move Windows WinFsp operations to `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/` (gap closure)\n- Delete ~5,900 lines of orphaned/duplicated desktop code\n- Security hardening: enable `zeroize` feature on `ed25519-dalek`, wrap keypair generation in `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`\n\n## Crate Architecture\n\n```\ncipherbox-crypto  ← AES-GCM, AES-CTR, ECIES, HKDF, Ed25519\ncipherbox-core    ← folder/file/bin metadata, vault blob v2, IPNS records\ncipherbox-api-client ← typed HTTP client for CipherBox API\ncipherbox-fuse    ← FUSE filesystem (macOS/Linux/Windows platform modules)\ncipherbox-sdk     ← sync daemon, upload queue, state management\n```\n\n## Requirements Completed\n\nRSDK-01 through RSDK-10 (all 10 Rust SDK Extraction requirements)\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `cargo check` across all 5 crates (macOS FUSE feature)\n- [x] `cargo test -p cipherbox-crypto` — 5 cross-language parity tests pass\n- [x] Zero `crate::fuse::` references remain in crate-side Windows files\n- [x] Desktop compiles against workspace crates (no duplicated logic)\n- [ ] CI: workspace builds on macOS, Linux, Windows\n- [ ] CI: cross-language parity gate (`scripts/check-vector-parity.sh`)\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",

}

        run: cargo test --workspace --no-default-features --features winfsp

  cargo-macos:
    name: Cargo Check & Test (macOS)
    needs: [changes, lint]
    if: |
      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
    runs-on: macos-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install FUSE-T
        run: |
          brew install --cask fuse-t
          sudo mkdir -p /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
          FUSE_T_PC="/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig/fuse-t.pc"
          if [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
            FUSE_T_PC=$(find /Library /usr/local /opt/homebrew -name "fuse-t.pc" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
          fi
          if [ -z "$FUSE_T_PC" ] || [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
            echo "::error::fuse-t.pc not found after FUSE-T installation"
            exit 1
          fi
          sudo cp "$FUSE_T_PC" /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
          sudo sed -i '' 's/^Version:.*/Version: 2.9.9/' /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc

      - name: Install Rust toolchain
        run: rustup default stable

      - uses: actions/cache@v4
--
        run: cargo test --workspace --no-default-features --features fuse
        env:
          PKG_CONFIG_PATH: /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig

  cargo-linux:
    name: Cargo Check, Test & Coverage (Linux)
    needs: [changes, lint]
    if: |
      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
    runs-on: ubuntu-22.04
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install system dependencies
        run: |
          sudo apt-get update
          sudo apt-get install -y \
            libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev \
            libayatana-appindicator3-dev \
            librsvg2-dev \
            libssl-dev \
            libxdo-dev \
            libfuse3-dev \
            pkg-config \
            build-essential

      - name: Install Rust toolchain
```

Excellent! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of all three crates. Let me compile my findings into a detailed report:

## Summary: Unit Test Coverage Analysis for CipherBox Rust Crates

Based on my thorough exploration of the cipherbox-crypto, cipherbox-core, and cipherbox-sdk crates, here's the breakdown of testable pure logic:

---

### **CRATE: cipherbox-crypto** (2.7 KLoC)

**Module Overview:** Pure cryptographic primitives with no external service dependencies.

#### Modules with Existing Tests:
- `tests/cross_language.rs` — Contains 5 integration tests loading shared test vectors:
  - `aes_gcm_cross_language()` — Cross-language AES-256-GCM parity tests
  - `ed25519_cross_language()` — Ed25519 signing/verification parity tests
  - Additional ECIES, HKDF, and IPNS name derivation cross-language tests

#### Modules WITHOUT Tests (Pure Logic, Highly Testable):

| Module | Key Functions | Test Candidates | Why |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|-----|
| **aes.rs** | `encrypt_aes_gcm()`, `decrypt_aes_gcm()`, `seal_aes_gcm()`, `unseal_aes_gcm()` | Round-trip encryption/decryption, empty plaintext handling, invalid key/IV sizes, sealed format validation | Pure deterministic crypto; no external dependencies |
| **aes_ctr.rs** | `encrypt_aes_ctr()`, `decrypt_aes_ctr()`, `decrypt_aes_ctr_range()` | Counter computation correctness, arbitrary byte range decryption, boundary conditions, off-by-one errors | Random-access range decryption is complex math; edge cases: start_byte=0, end_byte at block boundary, range beyond data |
| **ecies.rs** | `wrap_key()`, `unwrap_key()` | Round-trip wrapping/unwrapping, invalid key format validation (bad prefix, wrong length), minimum ciphertext size check | Format validation is error-prone; test malformed keys |
| **ed25519.rs** | `generate_ed25519_keypair()`, `sign_ed25519()`, `verify_ed25519()`, `get_public_key()` | Deterministic public key derivation, signature verification (valid/invalid signatures), key size validation | All pure; verify() should never throw (handles errors gracefully) |
| **hkdf.rs** | `derive_vault_ipns_keypair()`, `derive_vault_key_ipns_keypair()`, `derive_file_ipns_keypair()`, `derive_registry_ipns_keypair()`, `derive_bin_ipns_keypair()` | Determinism (same input = same output), file ID length validation (< 10 chars rejected), cross-function isolation (different info strings produce different keys) | HKDF output must be deterministic for cross-language compatibility; file_id validation is easy to miss |
| **ipns_name.rs** | `derive_ipns_name()`, `encode_libp2p_public_key()`, `encode_unsigned_varint()`, `encode_base36()` | Protobuf encoding correctness, varint edge cases (0, 127, 128, u64::MAX), base36 encoding (leading zeros, empty input), full CIDv1 format validation | Complex encoding logic: varint underflow/overflow, base36 algorithm correctness, leading zero handling |
| **utils.rs** | `hex_to_bytes()`, `bytes_to_hex()`, `mime_from_extension()`, `generate_uuid_v4()` | Hex round-trip, case-insensitive MIME type matching, UUID format validation | Simple but error-prone: case sensitivity, MIME type lookup coverage |

**High Priority Test Additions:**
1. **aes_ctr_range()** — Parametrized tests for 50+ boundary combinations (start_byte, end_byte, block alignment)
2. **ipns_name()** — Vector tests matching TypeScript (CIDv1 binary format is complex)
3. **hkdf derivations** — Determinism tests ensuring same key always produces same IPNS name
4. **ecies key validation** — Test all error paths (bad prefix, wrong lengths, undersize ciphertext)

---

### **CRATE: cipherbox-core** (1.2 KLoC)

**Module Overview:** Domain types, metadata encryption/decryption, IPNS record creation.

#### Modules with Existing Tests:
- `vault_blob.rs` — 11 unit tests in `#[cfg(test)]` block:
  - Round-trip serialization/deserialization
  - Version detection (v1 vs v2)
  - Header format validation
  - Cross-platform test vectors (matches TypeScript)
  - Error cases: empty blob, overflow, zero-length key

#### Modules WITHOUT Tests (Pure Logic, Highly Testable):

| Module | Key Functions | Test Candidates | Why |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|-----|
| **folder.rs** | `encrypt_folder_metadata()`, `decrypt_folder_metadata()`, `encrypt_file_metadata()`, `decrypt_file_metadata()` | Round-trip encryption, version validation (v2 required), JSON serialization format, bad folder_key handling, corrupted sealed data | Encryption/decryption round-trips; version check rejects v1 (good to test); JSON parsing errors |
| **file.rs** | (Re-exports from folder.rs) | (Same as folder.rs tests) | Thin wrapper; tests in folder module cover it |
| **ipns.rs** | `create_ipns_record()`, `build_cbor_data()`, `compute_v1_signature()`, `compute_v2_signature()`, `format_validity_timestamp()`, `civil_from_days()` | CBOR encoding format, RFC3339 timestamp formatting, signature correctness, date calculation edge cases (leap years, epoch boundaries), field ordering matches ipns npm package | Complex date math (civil_from_days); RFC3339 formatting with nanoseconds is finicky; CBOR field ordering must match JS package exactly |
| **bin.rs** | `encrypt_bin_metadata()`, `decrypt_bin_metadata()`, `empty_bin_metadata()` | Round-trip encryption, version validation (v1 required), empty bin creation | Similar to folder tests; ECIES round-trip |
| **registry.rs** | Type definitions only (DeviceEntry, DeviceRegistry, enums) | Serde serialization to camelCase JSON, enum variant names (pending/authorized/revoked lowercase, platform lowercase), optional field skipping | JSON field naming (camelCase); enum serialization case sensitivity; optional field behavior |
| **decrypt.rs** | `decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public()`, `decrypt_file_metadata_from_ipfs_public()` | IPFS JSON format parsing (IV hex + base64 data), IV length validation, base64 decoding errors, round-trip with unsealing | Hybrid format (JSON outer, base64+sealed inner); easy to introduce off-by-one in IV extraction |

**High Priority Test Additions:**
1. **ipns.rs** — RFC3339 formatting and date math (24+ test cases for boundary dates)
2. **folder.rs / file.rs** — Metadata round-trip tests (20+ vectors with different child counts)
3. **bin.rs** — Bin metadata round-trip with various entry types
4. **decrypt.rs** — IPFS public format decryption (bad IV hex, bad base64, IV length mismatch)

---

### **CRATE: cipherbox-sdk** (0.8 KLoC)

**Module Overview:** Stateful orchestration, queue management, sync daemon, registry operations.

#### Modules with Existing Tests:
- None identified (no `#[cfg(test)]` blocks)

#### Modules WITHOUT Tests (Pure Logic, Testable):

| Module | Key Functions | Test Candidates | Why |
|--------|--------------|-----------------|-----|
| **queue.rs** | `WriteQueue::new()`, `enqueue()`, `process()`, `len()`, `is_empty()` | FIFO ordering, retry counter increment, max_retries boundary (5 tries then drop), empty queue handling, item dequeuing order | Pure queue logic; easy to test with mock UploadHandler; important for offline scenarios |
| **state.rs** | `KeyState::new()`, `from_url()`, `clear()` | Key zeroing via `zeroize`, all fields cleared on logout, `clear()` idempotency | Memory safety; verify zeroize works; ensure no fields remain after clear |
| **sync.rs** | Sync sequence logic (not the async/await parts) — `cached_sequence_numbers` comparison, write queue integration points | Sequence number comparison (old=0 vs new=100), offline detection, write queue processing trigger conditions | Pure state comparison logic; skip async/await plumbing; focus on sequence number change detection |
| **registry.rs** | Type definitions (DeviceInfo); device entry construction logic | Cross-platform device info validation, DeviceAuthStatus assignment (first device = Authorized), platform enum handling | Data validation; enum assignment logic; first-device special case |
| **client.rs** | `CipherBoxSdkClient::new()`, `from_state()`, initial state | Constructor wiring, sync_handle initialization (None vs Some) | Simple factory pattern; verify initial state is correct |
| **error.rs** | Error type definitions | Serde serialization of SdkError variants | Error display messages are user-facing |

**High Priority Test Additions:**
1. **queue.rs** — Write queue FIFO ordering, retry logic, max_retries boundary (3-5 test cases)
2. **state.rs** — Key clearing/zeroization (verify sensitive fields are wiped)
3. **sync.rs** — Sequence number change detection (old=0, new=100 should trigger; old=100, new=100 should not)

---

## Summary Table: Test Coverage Status

| Crate | Total LoC | Modules | With Tests | Without Tests | Test Gap % |
|-------|-----------|---------|-----------|--------------|-----------|
| crypto | 914 | 9 | 1 (tests/) | 8 (src) | 89% |
| core | 1,146 | 6 | 1 (vault_blob) | 5 | 83% |
| sdk | 821 | 6 | 0 | 6 | 100% |
| **TOTAL** | **2,881** | **21** | **2** | **19** | **90%** |

---

## Recommended Unit Test Priorities

### Phase 1 (Highest ROI):
1. **crypto/aes_ctr.rs::`decrypt_aes_ctr_range()`** — Complex byte range math; 50+ edge cases
2. **crypto/ipns_name.rs** — CIDv1 base36 encoding; cross-language compatibility required
3. **core/ipns.rs** — RFC3339 timestamp formatting; date math edge cases
4. **sdk/queue.rs** — FIFO queue behavior; retry logic

### Phase 2 (Medium Priority):
1. **crypto/hkdf.rs** — HKDF determinism; file ID validation
2. **crypto/ecies.rs** — Key format validation
3. **core/folder.rs** — Metadata round-trip encryption
4. **core/decrypt.rs** — IPFS format parsing

### Phase 3 (Nice to Have):
1. **core/bin.rs** — Recycle bin round-trips
2. **core/registry.rs** — Serde camelCase serialization
3. **sdk/state.rs** — Key zeroization
4. **sdk/sync.rs** — Sequence number comparison logic

---

returns error (GCM tag validation)\n- Empty plaintext round-trip\n- Invalid key size (not 32 bytes) returns error\n- Invalid IV size (not 12 bytes) returns error\n\n### 2. crates/crypto/src/aes_ctr.rs\nTest:\n- encrypt_aes_ctr / decrypt_aes_ctr round-trip\n- decrypt_aes_ctr_range with various byte ranges:\n  - Full range (0..len)\n  - Partial range starting at 0\n  - Partial range starting mid-block (offset 7)\n  - Range spanning multiple blocks (offset 100, len 500)\n  - Single byte range\n  - Empty range (start == end should return empty)\n- Invalid key size returns error\n- Invalid IV size (not 16 bytes) returns error\n\n### 3. crates/crypto/src/ecies.rs\nTest:\n- wrap_key / unwrap_key round-trip with generated keypair\n- unwrap_key with wrong private key returns error\n- wrap_key with invalid public key returns error\n- unwrap_key with truncated ciphertext returns error\n\n### 4. crates/crypto/src/ed25519.rs\nTest:\n- generate_ed25519_keypair produces 32-byte keys\n- sign_ed25519 / verify_ed25519 round-trip\n- verify_ed25519 with wrong message returns false\n- verify_ed25519 with wrong key returns false\n- sign_ed25519 with invalid key size returns error\n- get_public_key determinism (same private key → same public key)\n\n### 5. crates/crypto/src/hkdf.rs\nTest:\n- Each derive_*_ipns_keypair function returns 32-byte keys\n- Determinism: same input produces same output\n- Different info strings produce different outputs (derive_vault vs derive_file)\n- derive_file_ipns_keypair with file_id < 10 chars returns error\n- derive_bin_ipns_keypair with file_id < 10 chars returns error\n\n### 6. crates/crypto/src/ipns_name.rs\nTest:\n- derive_ipns_name produces string starting with \"k\"\n- Same public key always produces same IPNS name\n- Different public keys produce different names\n- encode_unsigned_varint edge cases: 0, 1, 127, 128, 255, 300, 16384\n- encode_base36 round-trip validation\n\n### 7. crates/crypto/src/utils.rs\nTest:\n- hex_to_bytes / bytes_to_hex round-trip\n- hex_to_bytes with odd-length string returns error\n- hex_to_bytes with invalid hex chars returns error\n- generate_file_key returns 32 bytes\n- generate_iv returns 12 bytes\n- generate_random_bytes returns correct length\n- clear_bytes zeros all bytes\n\n## Rules:\n- Add tests as #[cfg(test)] mod tests { ... } at the bottom of each file\n- Use `use super::*;` to import from parent module\n- Keep tests concise — no comments explaining obvious things\n- Use assert_eq!, assert_ne!, assert!(matches!(...)) for error types\n- Do NOT modify any non-test code\n- Do NOT create new files — only edit existing ones\n- Run `cargo test -p cipherbox-crypto` after all edits to verify tests pass\n",

    58→# Patched fuser with loop-read for FUSE-T socket compatibility.
    59→# FUSE-T communicates via Unix domain socket (not /dev/fuse), so large
    60→# FUSE messages can arrive in fragments. Stock fuser does a single read()
    61→# and fails with "Short read of FUSE request". This patch loop-reads
    62→# until the full message is received.

> AGENT

Found it. The `[patch.crates-io]` for fuser is at the workspace level, meaning it applies to ALL members. On Windows, when `--features winfsp` is used, neither the desktop nor the fuse crate requests fuser as a dependency — but the vendored fuser's `build.rs` still runs because Cargo resolves the patched crate. The vendored fuser's build script requires `libfuse` headers which don't exist on Windows.

The fix: make the `[patch.crates-io]` conditional on Unix, or gate the vendored fuser's build script. The simplest approach is to make the patch only apply on cfg(unix). But `[patch]` doesn't support cfg conditions. Instead, I need to fix the vendored fuser's build script to be a no-op on Windows.

> TOOL

  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/fuser/build.rs"
}
```

     1→fn main() {
     2→    // Register rustc cfg for switching between mount implementations.
     3→    // When fuser MSRV is updated to v1.77 or above, we should switch from 'cargo:' to 'cargo::' syntax.
     4→    println!(
     5→        "cargo:rustc-check-cfg=cfg(fuser_mount_impl, values(\"pure-rust\", \"libfuse2\", \"libfuse3\"))"
     6→    );
     7→
     8→    #[cfg(all(not(feature = "libfuse"), not(target_os = "linux")))]

    10→
    11→    #[cfg(not(feature = "libfuse"))]
    12→    {
    13→        println!("cargo:rustc-cfg=fuser_mount_impl=\"pure-rust\"");
    14→    }
    15→    #[cfg(feature = "libfuse")]
    16→    {
    17→        if cfg!(target_os = "macos") {
    18→            if pkg_config::Config::new()
    19→                .atleast_version("2.6.0")
    20→                .probe("fuse") // for macFUSE 4.x
    21→                .map_err(|e| eprintln!("{e}"))
    22→                .is_ok()
    23→            {
    24→                println!("cargo:rustc-cfg=fuser_mount_impl=\"libfuse2\"");
    25→                println!("cargo:rustc-cfg=feature=\"macfuse-4-compat\"");
    26→            } else {
    27→                pkg_config::Config::new()
    28→                    .atleast_version("2.6.0")
    29→                    .probe("osxfuse") // for osxfuse 3.x
    30→                    .map_err(|e| eprintln!("{e}"))
    31→                    .unwrap();
    32→                println!("cargo:rustc-cfg=fuser_mount_impl=\"libfuse2\"");
    33→            }
    34→        } else {
    35→            // First try to link with libfuse3
    36→            if pkg_config::Config::new()
    37→                .atleast_version("3.0.0")
    38→                .probe("fuse3")
    39→                .map_err(|e| eprintln!("{e}"))
    40→                .is_ok()
    41→            {
    42→                println!("cargo:rustc-cfg=fuser_mount_impl=\"libfuse3\"");
    43→            } else {
    44→                // Fallback to libfuse
    45→                pkg_config::Config::new()
    46→                    .atleast_version("2.6.0")
    47→                    .probe("fuse")
    48→                    .map_err(|e| eprintln!("{e}"))
    49→                    .unwrap();
    50→                println!("cargo:rustc-cfg=fuser_mount_impl=\"libfuse2\"");
    51→            }
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}

> AGENT

Line 8-9: `#[cfg(all(not(feature = "libfuse"), not(target_os = "linux")))]` calls `unimplemented!()`. On Windows with no libfuse feature, this panics. The fix is to make the build script a no-op on Windows, since fuser is a Unix-only crate.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_014P7MaDXZ2YwELiCApFkrEz
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/fuser/build.rs",
  "old_string": "fn main() {\n    // Register rustc cfg for switching between mount implementations.\n    // When fuser MSRV is updated to v1.77 or above, we should switch from 'cargo:' to 'cargo::' syntax.\n    println!(\n        \"cargo:rustc-check-cfg=cfg(fuser_mount_impl, values(\\\"pure-rust\\\", \\\"libfuse2\\\", \\\"libfuse3\\\"))\"\n    );\n\n    #[cfg(all(not(feature = \"libfuse\"), not(target_os = \"linux\")))]\n    unimplemented!(\"Building without libfuse is only supported on Linux\");",
  "new_string": "fn main() {\n    // Register rustc cfg for switching between mount implementations.\n    // When fuser MSRV is updated to v1.77 or above, we should switch from 'cargo:' to 'cargo::' syntax.\n    println!(\n        \"cargo:rustc-check-cfg=cfg(fuser_mount_impl, values(\\\"pure-rust\\\", \\\"libfuse2\\\", \\\"libfuse3\\\"))\"\n    );\n\n    // fuser is Unix-only. On Windows the crate is resolved by [patch.crates-io]\n    // but never actually used (WinFSP replaces it). Skip the build script entirely.\n    #[cfg(target_os = \"windows\")]\n    return;\n\n    #[cfg(all(not(feature = \"libfuse\"), not(target_os = \"linux\")))]\n    unimplemented!(\"Building without libfuse is only supported on Linux\");"
}
```

> AGENT

Line 102 is clearly wrong — `device_info` was partially moved into `device_entry` on lines 69-86 (fields `name`, `platform`, `app_version`, `device_model` are moved). So `device_info.app_version` is already consumed. The `device_id` is still available via `.clone()`, but it's the wrong field.

I need to store `app_version` and `device_model` before the move.

The root issue: `[patch.crates-io]` makes the vendored fuser available to the entire workspace. Even though no crate explicitly depends on fuser when `winfsp` feature is active, Cargo still resolves and checks the patched crate.

> AGENT

Both have fuser as optional. The issue is that `[patch.crates-io]` causes Cargo to compile the patched crate even when it's optional and not activated — this is a known Cargo behavior with workspace patches.

The cleanest fix: make the `[patch.crates-io]` conditional on target OS by using a `cfg` in the vendored fuser's `lib.rs` to be a no-op on Windows. But actually, fuser has tons of Unix-only code throughout. The real solution is to prevent Cargo from compiling it on Windows.

Let me check if there's an alternative approach — can I make fuser a `[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]` in both crates?

> AGENT

The problem is that fuser is declared as a top-level optional dependency, not a target-gated one. Cargo resolves `[patch.crates-io]` for ANY crate that mentions fuser, regardless of whether the feature is active. On Windows, when `--no-default-features --features winfsp` is used, fuser shouldn't be compiled — but the patch forces resolution.

The simplest fix: move fuser under `[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]` in both Cargo.tomls. Optional deps under target-specific sections are only compiled on matching targets.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01BoVhRJJVgdDge8D1Mg1zKp
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/crates/fuse/Cargo.toml",
  "old_string": "# Platform-specific (Unix FUSE)\nfuser = { version = \"0.16\", default-features = false, features = [\"libfuse\"], optional = true }\nunicode-normalization = { workspace = true, optional = true }\n\n# Platform-specific (Windows)\nwinfsp = { version = \"0.12\", optional = true, features = [\"system\"] }\nwidestring = { version = \"1\", optional = true }\n\n[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]\nlibc = \"0.2\"",
  "new_string": "# Platform-specific (Windows)\nwinfsp = { version = \"0.12\", optional = true, features = [\"system\"] }\nwidestring = { version = \"1\", optional = true }\n\n[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]\nfuser = { version = \"0.16\", default-features = false, features = [\"libfuse\"], optional = true }\nunicode-normalization = { workspace = true, optional = true }\nlibc = \"0.2\""
}
```

     1→//! macOS-specific FUSE mount/unmount using FUSE-T with SMB backend.
     2→
     3→use crate::mount_point;
     4→
     5→/// Unmount the FUSE filesystem on macOS.
     6→///
     7→/// Calls `umount` first, then `diskutil unmount force` as fallback.
     8→pub fn unmount_filesystem() -> Result<(), String> {
     9→    let mount_path = mount_point();
    10→    log::info!("Unmounting CipherBoxFS at {}", mount_path.display());
    11→
    12→    // Clean up temp directory
    13→    let temp_dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("cipherbox");
    14→    if temp_dir.exists() {
    15→        if let Err(e) = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&temp_dir) {
    16→            log::warn!("Failed to clean temp directory: {}", e);
    17→        }
    18→    }
    19→
    20→    let status = std::process::Command::new("umount")
    21→        .arg(mount_path.to_str().unwrap())
    22→        .status()
    23→        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to run umount: {}", e))?;
    24→
    25→    if status.success() {
    26→        log::info!("FUSE filesystem unmounted successfully");
    27→        Ok(())
    28→    } else {
    29→        log::info!("umount failed (likely busy), trying diskutil unmount force");
    30→        let status = std::process::Command::new("diskutil")
    31→            .args(["unmount", "force", mount_path.to_str().unwrap()])
    32→            .status()
    33→            .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to run diskutil unmount force: {}", e))?;
    34→
    35→        if status.success() {
    36→            log::info!("FUSE filesystem force-unmounted via diskutil");
    37→            Ok(())
    38→        } else {
    39→            Err(format!(
    40→                "Failed to unmount {} -- close Finder windows and retry",
    41→                mount_path.display()
    42→            ))
    43→        }
    44→    }
    45→}
    46→
